                torque)

              F.1.4.2. Interval Key Issues

          Swab possibility
          Special care must be taken when the BHA is inside the previous casing. In contrary to

          popular belief, pressure does travel easily in drilling fluids, so tripping speed must be

           controlled to avoid swabbing.

          Stuck Pipe
        The combination of different size of CARBO-WATE incorporated into the mud will form a

           thin and slick filter cake in borehole and prevent the risk of stuck pipe.

           Drilling Issues
       When drilling through fractured lime stones, swabbing of hydrocarbons can be experienced.

           Influx due to overpressure vs. influx due to swabbing must be differentiated.

           Losses
          Pressure differences between the different reservoir zones in this section can cause  lost

           circulation problems. It is recommended to drill with as low as possible mud weight through

          the reservoir.

        March 2016                       Page 115 of 223         Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan

                  APP-04 Drilling Program

Doc No.: NDCAZD-EN-WP-004-0            Mud & Cement Program                    Contract No.: MTN-94-854

          H2S & CO2 Gases
         There is possibility for existence of H2S and CO2 Gases in the reservoir zons (Asmari,Pabdeh,

         Ilam and Sarvak), it is recommended to keep the pH around 9.5 to 10 and also using H2S SCAV

        P to prevent mud properties from being changed.

              F.1.4.3. Engineering Outline & System Maintenance

          Mud Weight
      A mud weight of 75-84 pcf will be used to drill this section, a mixture of Calcium Carbonate

            different grades will be used to reduce the filter cake thickness. It is preferred to use CARBO-

      WATE bridging agent rather than the conventional Calcium Carbonate.

          Rheology
       Mud Rheology was justified to ensure maximum hole cleaning in both dynamic and static

           conditions. Using BIO-XCD/ PAC UHV would help to adjust the YP and 6/3 RPM.

       PV is always to be kept as low as possible by reducing the drilled solids concentrations of mud

          using the proper installation of the solids control equipment so that the hydraulics is optimized.

       YP is selected to be in the programmed range. High YP values are recommended to reduce the

          annular flow rates required and consequently less hole erosion.

           Shale Inhibition
        To provide maximum inhibition during drilling this section, KCL R-UPG concentration should

         be kept at 3%-5% Wt and GLYCO-DRILL and CLAY-BOND L should be added before

           entering to the shale interval.

          pH
         During normal drilling operations, pH should be kept above a minimum of 9.5 in an attempt to

          minimize polymer degradation, to neutralize any H2S gas in the mud and to reduce the

          corrosion rate of the drill string.

          API Fluid Loss
          This hole section will be drilled using ULTRA-INHIBIT FLUID SYSTEM. It is recommended

           to maintain the fluid loss below 3 cc/30 min using FL control polymers to minimize the filter

         cake thickness.

           Reduced Dilution Rates
       GLYCO-DRILL GP improves the inhibition and stability of clays and shales and field data has

        shown that much lower dilution rates are required to maintain optimum mud properties. In

        some cases, the fluid can even be recycled to give further cost and environmental savings.

        March 2016                       Page 116 of 223         Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan

                  APP-04 Drilling Program

Doc No.: NDCAZD-EN-WP-004-0            Mud & Cement Program                    Contract No.: MTN-94-854

       GLYCO-DRILL GP improves filter cake quality and reduces fluid loss as the cloud point

          threshold is exceeded, the micro-emulsion formed by the now insoluble Glyco-Drill will help

           to reduce the HPHT fluid loss. This improvement in filter cake quality and the lower fluid loss

           will significantly reduce the chances of differential sticking.

         F.1.4.5. Lesson Learned and Recommendation
         These recommendation were prepared based on lesson learned of previous drilled wells, they

         can be useful to drill this well if used.

               Previous Wells show  rheological drop may be occurred while  drilling Asmari

                 Formation. Keeping YP in desired range avoids probable washout in this formation.

               Gas and oil influx have been recorded in previous wells, mud weight should be designed

                   to prevent influx in Gurpi and Ilam formations.

               The combination of different size of CARBO-WATE incorporated into the mud will

               form a thin and slick filter cake in borehole and prevent the risk of stuck pipe.

               High level of inhibition is critical while drilling Pabdeh, Gurpi, Laffan and Kazhdomi

                  formations. Sloughing shale has been recorded in previous wells.

               Due to the importance of drilling this section, after drilling each stand in shale and clay

                   intervals the same stand should be Wash and Ream completely before connection and

                 continuing drilling.

                It is recommended to maintain the fluid loss below 3 cc/30 min using the FL control

                polymers to minimize the filter cake thickness.

               Loss circulations may happen during drilling Ilam and Sarvak Formations. In case of

                 happening, LCM pills should be prepared and pumped to cure the loss circulation.

             F.1.5.1  Drilling Fluids Formulation

        SHALE-DRILL FLUID SYSTEM has been design to  drill this section for increasing the

       ROP during drilling by reducing friction factor.

             F.1.5.2  Interval Key Issues

          Swab possibility
          Special care must be taken when the BHA is inside the previous casing. In contrary to

          popular belief, pressure does travel easily in drilling fluids, so tripping speed must be

           controlled to avoid swabbing.

           Drilling Issues
       When drilling through fractured lime stones, swabbing of hydrocarbons can be experienced.

          Influx due to overpressure vs. influx due to swabbing must be differentiated.

           Losses
          Pressure differences between the different reservoir zone in this section can cause lost

           circulation problems. It is recommended to drill with as low as possible mud weight through

          the reservoir.

          H2S & CO2 Gases
         There is possibility for existence of H2S and CO2 Gases in the reservoir, it is recommended

           to keep the pH around 9.5 to 10 and also using H2S SCAV P to prevent mud properties from

          being changed.

             F.1.5.3 Engineering Outline & System Maintenance

           Shale Inhibition
        To provide maximum inhibition during drilling this section, KCL R-UPG concentration

          should be kept at 5% Wt and GLYCO-DRILL and ASPHA SHALE NHP should be added to

          system.

          pH
         During normal drilling operations, pH should be kept above a minimum of 9.0 in an attempt

           to minimize polymer degradation, to neutralize any H2S gas in the mud and to reduce the

          corrosion rate of the drill string.

        March 2016                       Page 120 of 223         Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan

                  APP-04 Drilling Program

Doc No.: NDCAZD-EN-WP-004-0            Mud & Cement Program                    Contract No.: MTN-94-854

          API Fluid Loss
          This hole section will be drilled using SHALE-DRILL FLUID SYSTEM. It is recommended

           to maintain the fluid loss below 3 cc/30 min using FL control polymers to minimize the filter

         cake thickness.

           Reduced Dilution Rates
       GLYCO-DRILL GP improves the inhibition and stability of clays and shales and field data has

        shown that much lower dilution rates are required to maintain optimum mud properties. In

        some cases, the fluid can even be recycled to give further cost and environmental savings.

       GLYCO-DRILL GP improves filter cake quality and reduces fluid loss as the cloud point

          threshold is exceeded, the micro-emulsion formed by the now insoluble Glyco-Drill will help

           to reduce the HPHT fluid loss. This improvement in filter cake quality and the lower fluid loss

           will significantly reduce the chances of differential sticking.

             F.1.5.4 Lesson Learned and Recommendation

         These recommendation were prepared based on lesson learned of previous drilled wells, they

         can be useful to drill this well if used.

               Due to the importance of drilling this section, after drilling each stand in shale and clay

                   intervals the same stand should be Wash and Ream completely before connection and

                 continuing drilling.

               Adequate shale and clay inhibition is necessary during drilling this section.

            F.1.5. Completion Fluids Design and Program

            F. 1.5.1. Clean up and Completion Program

          This program describes the procedure to clean up and displace the cased hole from water based

       mud brine as safely and effectively as possible prior to running the completion equipment.

         Clean working practices and good housekeeping as always are important.

          All rig pits, ditches, and lines (including gun lines) should be cleaned using solvents and

           detergents.  If it is practical, lines should be opened to check for any mud solids that might have

            settled in them.

          All the pits, sand traps, and under the shale shakers should be cleaned out and washed down using

          high pressure cleaning equipment. In the pit room, all gratings should be cleaned, all the lights

         and beams should be washed down.

          All cleaning should be done well in advance wherever possible. To clean pits and lines, 15 m3

          of water containing 1 to 2 drums of CLEAN SURF should be mixed up and circulated through

             all lines, gun lines, mixing lines etc. This can also be used to clean the tank system initially.

               Pump should be worked at the maximum safe rate.

               The below check list should be filled by the supervisor in the site.

                   Equipment                     Complete

                      Brine Storage Tanks

                       Transfer Lines

                            Pill and Slug Pits

                      Reserve/Mix Pit

                     Rig Pumps

                       Trip Tank

                 Pump Charge Lines

                   Cement Unit Lines

                   Choke Unit Lines

                           Kill Lines

                It should be noted that all dump valves should seal with grease to ensure they are in good

                order.

        March 2016                       Page 123 of 223         Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan

                  APP-04 Drilling Program

Doc No.: NDCAZD-EN-WP-004-0            Mud & Cement Program                    Contract No.: MTN-94-854

               All ditch gates should be sealed with silicon on each side of the gate. This will need to be

               replaced if the gate is opened.

               Under no circumstances should barite, bentonite or polymers be used to seal anything.

               All pump packing should be examined before starting the cleaning.

              F.1.5.2. Displacement Design

        To ensure effective mud displacement and water wetting of the casing, the displacement pill needs

           to be designed carefully. The use of a weighted push pills with a viscosity greater than that of the

         mud, will minimize channeling and ensure good cleaning of the low side of the well bore.

        The best fluid for thinning the mud is the continuous phase of the mud. In addition, SPERSEEN

       CFL could be used to reduce the yield point and gel strengths. The yield point should be between

         8 to10 Pa and the 10 minute gel to <10 Pa. The actual quantity of material required to do this

         must be determined by the pilot testing performed by the mud engineer to determine the most

           cost-effective treatment.

         Throughout the drilling fluid conditioning process, the flow rate used should be the maximum

           practical rate.

               The drill string should be reciprocated and rotated during the operation. The combined

                  effect of scrapers being run in the hole, the high flow rate and the reciprocation/rotation will

                help to remove/disperse large quantities of mud solids and general debris. The reciprocation

                 stroke should be +/- 10 m. and the rotation speed should be +/- 60 RPM

               During this operation, as much of the surface volume of mud as possible should be

              removed from the pits to accommodate the inhibited water and the well clean up pills. Once

                 the mud has been back-loaded, surface preparation can be done.

               At least 150 m of pill/spacer coverage or 3 min contact time at the highest pump rates

               should be considered.

               Pit levels should be monitor throughout the displacement operation to catch and prevent

              an influx entering the well contributing to an incident.

              North Drilling Company

       K.1. Well Summary and Introduction

                                        Drilling Section Interval
                  Hole Size (in)                     Mud Weight (pcf)
                                          (m)

                       26                 151                    65-68

                     17 1/2               1105                   67-79

                     12 1/4                299                   90-105

                      8 1/2                2403                   75-84

                      5 7/8                342                   95-110

                    Total Depth             4300                              -

        The main purpose of the waste management system is to keep the location as clean as possible

         from the drilling waste. The system collects the waste materials from the drilling operations

         and separates the solids from the liquids in order to have a final disposition from the liquid

         phase as well as the solids produced. For the Waste Management system, all the cuttings

         coming from the  shakers, desander,  desilter,  solid  control centrifuges and dewatering

            centrifuge/filter press are collected and treated. Also all the waste fluids such as mud and water

           are collected and sent to dewatering in order to have a good separation of solids from water.

        The main part of drilling waste is waste water and drilling fluid waste so the main part of

            drilling waste management is reducing water consumption and treating drilling fluid waste

         which is a very serious threat for nature.

          February 2016                      Page 153 of 223        Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan

                  APP-04 Drilling Program

Doc No.: NDCAZD-EN-WP-004-0             Waste Management Program                 Contract No.: MTN-94-854

            K.2.2. Dewatering System

         Dewatering system includes a contained flocculation unit with pumps, polymer preparation

          tank and mixing system, and a Filter Press. Basically the dewatering system is the separation

          of the solid and liquid phases of the slurry, using mechanical and chemical. The liquid is

           collected in the coral and sent to the buffer tank #1 (or feed pump). It is then pumped to the

          dewatering unit and on the way to the filter press, a coagulant (Poly Aluminum Chloride

         (PAC)) and a flocculent polymer (Poly Electrolyte) are injected to the slurry. The mixture of

          February 2016                      Page 154 of 223        Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan

                  APP-04 Drilling Program

Doc No.: NDCAZD-EN-WP-004-0             Waste Management Program                 Contract No.: MTN-94-854

           the coagulant, flocculent and the fluid goes inside of the filter press where the solid and liquid

          phases are separated using the filter cartridges of the filter press. As a result, treated water is

          received from the filter press and collected in buffer tank #2 while dumping the solids to the

            corral.

        The Dewatering suction lines could be connected to middle mud pit and all DWM buffer tanks.

          In order to use the dewatered water for making new mud and to avoid settling of solids in new

         mud, using as little flocculent material as possible is considered. Dewatered water is tested by

      NDCO personnel in order to verify the quality; if water is going to be used to prepare new mud,

       mud engineer must perform a compatibility pilot test prior using it, if test failed, water must be

           retreated and then the pilot test must be done again till the water is suitable for the mud.

            K.2.3. Filter Press

      A filter press is a tool used in separation processes, specifically in solid/liquid separation using

           the principle of pressure drive, provided by a slurry pump. The filter press is used in fixed-

         volume and batch operations, which means that the operation must be stopped to discharge the

             filter cake before the next batch can be started. The major components of a filter press are the

          skeleton and the filter pack. The skeleton holds the filter pack together while pressure is being

         developed inside the filtration chamber. The chamber however can only hold a specific volume

          of solids.

            K.2.4. Buffer Tanks

       Two rectangle tanks with 400-450 BBL capacities were supplied and installed by NDCO for

     DWM purposes. Tanks were used as per requirements to store treated water from dewatering

         system or collected from the pits; water stored in the tanks was pumped back to the rig water

          tanks to be recycled to be used for cleaning purposes or to prepare new mud.

            K.2.5. Pump Transfer Package

       Pump  transfer package  consist of two  centrifugal pumps, two diaphragm pumps, two

          submersible pumps and one vacuum pump. Pumps are available for transferring all fluids from

           the pits, tanks, cellar, corral and oil trap as per requirements.

          February 2016                      Page 155 of 223        Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan

                  APP-04 Drilling Program

Doc No.: NDCAZD-EN-WP-004-0             Waste Management Program                 Contract No.: MTN-94-854

            K.2.6. Loader

        The loader is used to mix fixation and stabilization chemicals with cuttings. Also it is used to

          load truck with fixed cuttings for transportation to disposal area.

            K.2.7. Truck

        The truck is used for transfer fixed cuttings from well site to disposal area.

            K.2.8. Tanker Truck

        The tanker truck is used for transfer refined water from well site to disposal area.

            K.2.9. Vertical Cutting Dryer

        The Vertical Cutting Dryer uses centrifugal force to dry drilled solids in oil or synthetic base

            fluids. It provides G-Force up to 400 and extracts liquid from cuttings.

            K.3.1. Senior Waste Management Engineer

        Job Description

      NDCO will supply one Senior Waste Management Engineer. The Senior Waste Management

          Engineer is in charge of all waste management and will optimize the zero discharge process to

         comply with client requirements. The Senior Waste Management Engineer shall report directly

           to the Project Engineer and to the client representatives where required. They will, also, on a

          constant basis, consult with the mud engineers, rig personnel and third party personnel that are

          involved in the drilling operation to optimize the process.

          Responsibilities

               Control all waste management activities as per client Waste Management Program.

               Prepare daily waste management report and submit to Drilling Supervisor.

                Insure that a minimum stock level of required chemicals are available at the rig site and

                  consult with the Project Engineer to maintain this stock.

                Direct and assist the Junior Waste Management Engineer in his responsibilities.

               Ensure a safe working environment where all members of the team understand their

                   responsibilities to remain safe and be aware of their co-workers safety according to

            NDCO, client  Safety Policy.

               Carry out safety audits and regular safety meetings to build a safety ‘culture’.

               Ensure the improvement and the motivation of the subordinate engineers in order that

                 they accomplish their job objectives promoting the TEAM WORK spirit.

                Direct and control environmental compliance with the customers contractual policies

                Direct and control the Companies waste management activities in accordance with the

             Company Policies and Procedures.

               Build up a strong solid and valuable relationship with the Customer at all levels.

       K.4. Waste Management Procedures

        The safe and environmentally sensitive treatment and disposal of waste water and cuttings

          contaminated with drilling fluid originating from the drilling activity.

        The purpose is to provide a standard operating procedure to instruct/guide DWM personnel in

           the correct methods used in cutting by fixation process.

            K.4.1. Waste Water Treatment, Handling and Disposal

        Method
          Treating waste water and mud by the dewatering unit.

         Waste water will be received from:
           1. Pump cooling water
           2. Dumping of pits
           3. Dumping of traps
           4. Drain lines
           5. Rain
           6. Rig wash water

                               Waste Water Treatment Requirements

                RESPONSIBLE PERSONS        EQUIPMENT REQUIRED

                                                                Dewatering Unit

                                                                                      Filter Press
                              Senior DWM engineer                   Storage Tank
                                Junior DWM engineer                  Pumps
                                                                   Coagulant

                                                                        Flocculants

         Action required:
              1-  Supervisor/Senior Engineer  to hold safety meeting with  all involved personnel.

               Conduct a toolbox meeting prior to all operations. Identify risk and apply all standard

          NDCO safety procedures. Ensure full PPE is worn and the crews are aware of their

                   roles and responsibilities. Review previous safety bulletin cards to determine specific

                  risks/hazards associated with this task.

              2- Equipment Check. Senior Engineer to inspect all equipment associated with the task

               and determines operational suitability and ‘fit for purpose’ status including all above

               and chemical inventories of coagulants and flocculants.

          February 2016                      Page 160 of 223        Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan

                  APP-04 Drilling Program

Doc No.: NDCAZD-EN-WP-004-0             Waste Management Program                 Contract No.: MTN-94-854

              3-  Fill the suction tank with waste water or fluid from the waste pit and/or mud pits using

                  the diaphragm or submersible pumps.  This tank acts like a buffer to provide steady

               volume for the flocculation/coagulation process.

              4- Use  agitator or  centrifugal pump  for  agitating the  fluid  in the tank  to ensure

              homogenous fluid for dewatering process and prevent settling of solids.

              5-  Test waste water specific gravity, pH and solids content.

              6-  If necessary adjust pH in the suction tank.

              7- Check level of polymer in the mixing funnel, fill up if necessary.

              8- Don’t run out of polymer and interrupt the dewatering process.

              9- Check level of coagulant in the drum, prepare if necessary according to test results.

             10- Check water supply.

             11- Start filter press.

             12- Start dewatering unit.

             13- Start feed pump.

             14- Start floc polymer pump and adjust polymer volume.

             15- If necessary, start coagulant pumps and add coagulant fluid to improve flocculation.

             16- Check floc result from the sample valve (trial and error procedure).

             17- If the test results are good (fast settling, large floc’s, clear separation) feed the waste

                water to filter press.

             18- The effluent discharge should run first back to the waste water pit or return to the

                  suction tank until all adjustments are made and the effluent is clean.

             19- Send clean effluent to the clear water pit/tank by using the T valve.

             20- When finished, stop all pumps and filter press.

             21- Clean dewatering unit after usage. Take care of polymer mixing parts.

             22- For cuttings generated in the dewatering process follow with Standard Operating

                 Procedures.

             23- Threated water send to tanks for using in mud made. Waste water from one of the

                 following method will be disinfection:

               o  Liming

               o  Aerated

               o  Soda ash

               o  Biocide

             24- Discharged water after dewatering should meet below standards:

          February 2016                      Page 161 of 223        Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan

                  APP-04 Drilling Program

Doc No.: NDCAZD-EN-WP-004-0             Waste Management Program                 Contract No.: MTN-94-854

                   pH = 7-9

                   COD < 200 mg/lit, BOD < 300 mg/lit

                   Ca2+ < 600 mg/lit

                    Hardness < 800 mg/lit

                    Turbidity < 1000 ntu

                    Color (Color unit, chromaticity) <75

                   IPS-E-SF-880 Standard

            K.4.2. Cutting Fixation Treatment

        Method
          Solids mixing in the cutting treatment area with cement and Sodium Silicate.

                                   Cutting Fixation Treatment Requirements
                   RESPONSIBLE PERSONS          EQUIPMENT REQUIRED

                                                                         Loader
                               Senior DWM engineer
                                                                   Cement
                                 Junior DWM engineer
                                                               Sodium Silicate
                                 Loader driver
                                                                                 Retort test
         Action required:
              1-  Senior engineer to hold safety meeting with all personnel involved. Conduct a toolbox

                meeting prior to all operations. Identify risk and apply all standard NDCO safety

                 procedures. Ensure full PPE  is worn and the crews are aware of their roles and

                    responsibilities.  Review  previous  safety  bulletin  cards  to  determine  specific

                  risks/hazards associated with this task.

              2- Weekly inspection of loader. Inspection should include driver skills, breaks, reverse

                  alarm,  lights, wheels,  etc. Problems should be reported. The senior engineer  is

                  responsible for the proper use and condition of the equipment!

              3-  Cuttings are received from the corral discharged on the treatment area into the

               compartments made with treated cuttings.

              4- Take a sample of the cutting to be treated and perform a retort test, record retort test

                    results in fixation table.

              5- The batch quantity of cement and sodium silicate will be calculated by using the cement

                 curve and put into the bucket.

          February 2016                      Page 162 of 223        Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan

                  APP-04 Drilling Program

Doc No.: NDCAZD-EN-WP-004-0             Waste Management Program                 Contract No.: MTN-94-854

              6- The bucket will be poured into on the cuttings and mixed until consistent (about 30

                 min). This mixing time allows the cuttings to activate with the sodium silicate and

               cement for sufficient time to achieve initial Fixation.

              7- An initial sheen test will be conducted after 4 hours curing.

              8-  If initial sheen test fail, additional cement and sodium silicate will be added and mixed

                   for another 15 minutes, the process is continued until the material passes the sheen test

               and the batch is deposited for curing.

              9- The mixture will be allowed to set for 8 hours more (from the end of the initial sheen

                      test). This time allows the cuttings to further activate with the sodium silicate and

               cement for sufficient time to achieve secondary Fixation. A second step sheen test will

                be conducted at 12 hours (from the end of the initial mixing time).

             10- If second sheen test fail, the batch will be reprocessed with more cement and sodium

                     silicate for another 15 minutes, the process is continued until the material passes the

                sheen test and the batch is deposited for curing.

             11- At this point a Can test will be done on the batch. A Can test is a simple field test that

                can provide an indication of free liquid. Results are recorded on the fixation table.

             12- If can test pass the batch will be moved to a designated area for permanent residence.

                This could be a liner pit or it could be an area where the material could be laid down,

               smoothed and allowed to set for future use.

             13- If can test fail an absorbent material or additional cement and sodium silicate need to

                be used and mixed for another 15 minutes, the process is continued until the material

                 passes the can test and the batch is deposited for curing.

             14- A third and final sheen test will be conducted 48 hours (from the time of the initial

                mixing) to ensure that the process results have been achieved.

             15- If third and final sheen test pass, the mixture will be continue to cure in the designated

                  area and time and pass note will be marked on the process sheet.

             16- It is unlikely that the batch will fail the 48 hour test, however in the event that the sheen

                     test fails, the material will be returned to the mixing corral, broken up and will be added

                   to new batches of cuttings and re-cycled.

          February 2016                      Page 163 of 223        Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────

                    South Azadegan

        L.1. General Quality, Health, Safety, and Environmental
      Requirements

        Company is committed to conducting operations in a manner which minimizes the risks to the

          health and safety of those involved in, and affected by, such operations. Company is also

         committed to conducting its operations in a manner which minimizes the impact on natural and

            ‘built’ environments in the area of operations.

          This  section  sets  out minimum  Quality,  Health,  Safety and  Environmental (QHSE)

          requirements for the Work that Contractor and subcontractors will be required to comply with

           whilst conducting operations for Company.

        Company may perform audits and assessments of Contractor’s and  its subcontractors’

         performance under the Contract. Company reserves the right without prior notice to conduct

           quality, health, safety, security and environmental audits of the Operations.

          Contractor shall at all times be responsible for performing the WORK in a manner so as to

           protect against loss of life, injury to people, damage to property and the environment. The

           detailed manner means and methods of performing the WORK shall be under the control and

           direction of Contractor.

           Prior to the start of Operations, certain QHSE processes must be completed. These QHSE

          processes include developing the final QHSE plan format and frequency of any QHSE audits,

          permitting and licensing and Company’s approvals. Contractor shall ensure that sufficient

          resources are dedicated to this task to ensure completion prior to mobilization.

        Company may at any time stop the WORK or any portion of the WORK without penalty to

        Company if the WORK is not conducted in accordance with the Quality, Health, Safety or

         Environmental guidelines specified in this Contract. Company reserves the right to require

          Contractor to remove any person from the Operations in the case of non- compliance with

       QHSE procedures. Company may also prohibit the use of equipment and plant in cases where

           that equipment or plant does not meet the required standards or is deemed unsafe.

          Contractor  shall ensure  that  activities  associated with  this Contract  are conducted  in

         compliance with applicable national and  local HSE laws and regulations, Contractor’s

          standards and Company’s requirements and IADC documents.

            L.2.2. Training and Competence

          Contractor shall ensure that its employees and those of its subcontractors are trained and

           guidelines. Contractor’s senior staff shall have received training in safety management.

            L.2.3. Accident/Incident Reporting

          Contractor shall report all incidents. Any hazardous incident / near miss involving Company,

           Contractor’s, or any third party personnel, plant or equipment, shall be reported to Company,

         whether or not injury to personnel or damage/loss to plant or equipment resulted. The following

           incidents shall be verbally reported immediately to the on-site Company Representative(s):

        damage to property or equipment belonging to Company, third parties, Contractor or  its

          subcontractors; injury to personnel of Company, third parties, subcontractors or Contractor;

          near misses which may have had serious potential consequences; environmental damage;

         media attention. An initial written report, containing factual information only, shall be prepared

         by Contractor and delivered to the Company Representative within forty- eight (48) hours of

          the incident. Company reserves the right to participate in the investigation of any incident

           arising out of performance of the WORK.

          Contractor and all subcontractors shall have an accident and incident reporting system, details

          of which shall be included in the QHSE plan.

          Contractor  shall maintain and forward to Company, a monthly summary of  its safety

          performance.

          All Contractor and subcontractor personnel and Company Representative shall attend safety

         meetings on a regular basis. Company Representative shall be given a copy of the minutes of

          the meetings.

        L.3. Environmental Management

           Contractor shall comply with any and all laws, rules and regulations of governmental agencies

          having jurisdiction, which now exist or may be promulgated during the term of the Contract,

            relating to the control and prevention of damage to the environment.

          Without prejudice to Item 3.1 above, Contractor shall ensure that all personnel use the highest

            prevailing standards of care and diligence to prevent unlawful disposal of all waste oil, water

          and other waste materials or fluids which may accumulate or be caused by Contractor’s

          performance of the Contract. Contractor shall not dispose of any objects, scrap, waste, rubbish,

          equipment, fluids or fuel into the surrounding area or allow anything to be stored loose where

                  it can be driven into surrounding areas by wind. Contractor shall take  all reasonable

           precautions to prevent pollution of any nature or kind from its performance of the WORK.

           Contractor shall have contingency plans and procedures in place for such events.

           In the event of any disposal prohibited by Item 3.2 above, whether by dumping, spilling,

           emission or otherwise arising from Contractor’s performance or non-performance of the

           Contract, Contractor shall be fully liable for and bear the costs of removal of any such item(s)

          dumped,  spilled, emitted or otherwise disposed regardless of the  sole, concurrent or

           contributory negligence or breach of duty (statutory or otherwise) of Company. In the event

           of any such disposal, Contractor shall immediately undertake all reasonable remedial and

            protective measures required in such circumstances.

           Contractor shall notify Company immediately with respect to any pollution, loss, damage,

           claim or demand (or occurrence which may give rise to same) resulting from the Contract.

           Contractor shall report to Company any incidents of non-compliance with legislative and

           regulatory environmental requirements that occur during the performance of the WORK.

          Without relieving Contractor of any of its obligations under Item 3.2, if Contractor does not

          promptly proceed to perform its obligations under this Item 3, Company may take part to any

           degree it deems necessary in the control and removal of any pollution, dumping, spillage or

           contamination which is the responsibility of Contractor under this Item 3.2, or which is due

            to any act or omission of Contractor. Contractor shall reimburse Company for costs arising

          from such actions of Company upon receipt of invoice from Company. Company shall have

            L.3.6. Management of environment protection on location

         In drilling operation， keep mud and sewage from overflowing. If it happens, clean up

                  timely.

        Mud chemicals shall be stored according to the standard and shall not be scattered

                around well site. If mud chemicals scatter while loading or unloading, clean it up at

                 once. The sack of mud chemicals shall be recovered. Do not dispose it on site.

        Enhance water management at well location and do not wash equipment with fresh

                  water. Stop water flowing for long time. Overhead tanks shall be utilized.

         Oil management shall be carefully at the well site. No oil dirt on site, no dripping and

                 leaking at oil tank and manifold system area.

         After the well is completed, reinforce the sewage pit ，solidify the disposed mud and

                have environment procedure fulfilled.

         Install fuel supply lines and keep them in good condition.

         All the drilling fluid materials shall be recovered and shall not be left at the well site

                    after completion.

        The environmental protection personnel from contractor and operator side shall check

               and accept the well site environment according to the regulation after completion. Only

                 well site environment is qualified, the well can be delivered.

         Transition Area for Hazardous Waste shall also be provided by each Contractor in his

                  allocated area,

        Company shall approve each area before use as well. The transition area of hazardous

                waste shall be:

                     Paved, fenced, marked and illuminated

                     Equipped with appropriate Fire Extinguisher and other emergency response

                      equipment (e.g. Eye-Wash and Emergency Shower, appropriate First Aid kits,

                           neutralizer substances etc.).

                     In compliance with the requirements of handling, transportation and storage of

                 before the start of operations; at this meeting Contractor will provide a specific QHSE

                 plan for Company’s review.

               The outcome of the meeting will be a list of action items, time for completion, and

                   responsibilities for completion and acceptance. Company will review the information

                 presented and will respond in a timely manner. Under no circumstances will the WORK

                     start before the specific QHSE plan has been approved by Company.

                Site Start-Up Meeting(s) - attended by all field personnel including subcontractor

                    to:

               o  Inform personnel of Company’s and Contractor’s QHSE requirements and

               o  Inform personnel of specific HSE hazards, mitigating measures and procedures
                          to be followed;
               o  Inform personnel, including subcontractor supervisors and personnel of their
                QHSE responsibilities;
               o  Review emergency response and contingency plans.

        L.8. Well Control

         Recording Reference

           All BOP tests, drills, function tests, any malfunctions, repair or maintenance to the mud

          system and well control equipment shall be recorded in the IADC daily reports and shall be

           signed by both the Drilling Contractor's Tool Pusher and Drilling Supervisor on the well site.

         They shall also be recorded in the Daily Drilling Report.

         Flow Check Procedure

        To avoid a kick situation, it is recommended that a flow check be conducted at specific times

           during the drilling operation.

          While drilling(any warning sign)

           Prior to tripping the drill string

          While tripping(any warning sign)

          While out of hole(continuously)

           Trip out:

               o  After pulling 5% of the drill pipe

               o  At the midpoint depth of the wellbore

               o  Prior to pulling the first stand of drill collars

        March 2016                    Page 190 of 223             Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan

                  APP-04 Drilling Program

Doc No.: NDCAZD-EN-WP-004-0             Health, Safety and Environment                Contract No.: MTN-94-854

               o  After all the drill string is out of hole.

           Trip in:

               o  Upon reaching surface casing shoe

               o  At midpoint of the wellbore

         Primary Control Reference

              It is the responsibility of the Drilling Contractor to keep the hole full at all times by using all

          the available equipment. The Drilling Supervisor shall monitor that the correct preventive

           practices are being implemented and/or conducted at all times.

         Extreme care shall be taken to monitor mud volume, drilling breaks and gas cut mud. The

      MACP (Maximum allowable casing pressure) shall be confirmed by the Drilling Supervisor,

           either after setting each new casing string or, whenever the density of the drilling mud changes.

        The MACP shall be clearly written on a kick control sheet that will be posted near the choke

          control panel.

        The predetermined distance at which the Tool Joint is to be pulled above the rotary should be

          posted on rig floor near the BOP control panel.

        The Driller is responsible for carefully measuring and recording the slow pump pressure.

         Awareness of these values is an important element in killing operations, in order to avoid

          formation breakdown.

         Take a measure and record the slow pump pressure at three different flow rates with all pumps

            at the following times:

        Once per tour.

        Whenever changes occur in the mud density/rheology.

        Whenever changes occur in the dimension and characteristics of the string, BHA, jet

                    size, jet plugged or jet lost, etc.

        March 2016                    Page 191 of 223             Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan

                  APP-04 Drilling Program

Doc No.: NDCAZD-EN-WP-004-0             Health, Safety and Environment                Contract No.: MTN-94-854

         Secondary Control Reference

       Any time a drilling break is noticed, drilling shall stop and a static monitoring of the well shall

         be carried out. Space-out for all Pipe Rams and Annular Preventer must be known to the Driller

            at all times to keep tool joints clear of annular preventer and rams.

         Kick Detection Procedure While Drilling

             If there is an indication of a possible kick occurring, the Driller shall follow the kick detection

          procedures outlined below:

         Stop drilling with the pump on, Pick up top drive to a predetermined space-out position, Shut

           off the pumps and check for well flow by visual examination on the bell nipple or flow line.

             If the well is not flowing:

         Immediately notify the rig manager, Drilling Supervisor and Toolpusher.

        The Drilling Supervisor will decide whether to circulate bottoms up or resume drilling.

              The penetration rate will be limited as long as the cause of the abnormal situation is

                undetermined. Always be aware that some hydrocarbons (gas or oil cut mud) may rise

                   to the surface. Ensure that the proper monitoring and safety systems all in place.

             If the well is flowing:

         Close the well as per the Shut-in Procedure.

         Immediately notify the Well site Drilling Supervisor, rig manager and Toolpusher.

      M.1. Introduction

          This document has been developed in line with concerns regarding Hydrogen Sulfide (H2S)

          operations customized for operations being on the APP-04.

          In General Hydrogen sulfide (H2S) is an extremely hazardous, toxic compound. It is a colorless,

         flammable gas that can be identified in relatively low concentrations, by a characteristic rotten

         egg odor. The gas occurs naturally in coal pits, sulfur springs, gas wells, and as a product of

          decaying sulfur-containing organic matter, particularly under low oxygen conditions. Industrial

          sources of hydrogen sulfide include petroleum and natural gas extraction and refining, pulp

         and paper manufacturing, rayon textile production, leather tanning, chemical manufacturing

         and waste disposal.

         Formation gas from Asmari, Sarvak, and Dariyan containing harmful hydrogen sulfide (H2S)

          has the risks to harm site personnel during the operation. From analysis the H2S content in

         Sarvak formation is 2436 ppm. All site personnel should pay more attention on H2S hazards

         and all H2S equipment should be in good condition.

      M.2. Scope & Purpose

           Identifying, classifying, and responding to different levels of H2S hazard, H2S safety training,

             drills in South Azadegan Project Well No. APP-04.

      M.3. General Characteristics of Hydrogen Sulfide

               Chemical Symbol       H2S

                  Specific Gravity           1.192 (Heavier Than Air)

                Explosive Limits         Lower Limit: 4% - Upper Limit: 44% , Volume in air

                 Ignition Temperature     260o C

                  Solubility in Water         2.9 Volumes of gas per volume of water at 20o C

        March 2016                      Page 195 of 223          Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan

                  APP-04 Drilling Program

Doc No.: NDCAZD-EN-WP-004-0             H2S Monitoring & Control                   Contract No.: MTN-94-854

         Hydrogen sulfide has a very low odor threshold, with its smell being easily perceptible at

          concentrations well below 1 part per million (ppm) in air. The odor increases as the gas

         becomes more concentrated, with the strong rotten egg smell recognizable up to 30 ppm. Above

            this level, the gas is reported to have a sickeningly sweet odor up to around 100 ppm. However,

            at concentrations above 100 ppm, a person's ability to detect the gas is affected by rapid

         temporary paralysis of the olfactory nerves in the nose, leading to a loss of the sense of smell.

          This means that the gas can be present at dangerously high concentrations, with no perceivable

          odor. Prolonged exposure to lower concentrations can also result in similar effects of olfactory

           fatigue. This unusual property of hydrogen sulfide makes it extremely dangerous to rely totally

        on the sense of smell to warn of the presence of the gas.

                                          Physiological response

      Rank  %Weight    % Effect

              10 ppm            Slight conjunctivitis and respiratory tract

               50-100 ppm        irritation after 1 hour exposure

                               Coughing, eye irritation, loss of sense of smell after 2-15 minutes.
                                  Altered respiration, pain in the eyes and drowsiness after 15-30
              100 ppm         minutes followed by throat irritation after 1 hour. Several hours’
                                exposure results in gradual increase in severity of these symptoms
                               and death may occur within the next 48 hours.

                            Marked conjunctivitis and respiratory tract irritation after 1 hour of
              200-300 ppm
                                exposure

              500-700 ppm     Loss of consciousness and possibly death in 30 minutes to 1 hour.

              700-1000 ppm    Rapid unconsciousness, cessation of respiration and death.

                                Unconsciousness at once, with early cessation of respiration and
              1000-2000 ppm   death in a few minutes. Death may occur even if individual is
                             removed to fresh air at once.

         Hydrogen Sulfide is a naturally occurring gas mixed with natural gas or dissolved in the oil or

          brine and released upon exposure to atmospheric conditions.

        March 2016                      Page 196 of 223          Drilling Engineering Department

      M.4. Plan

          Contractor shall provide the H2S monitoring and control services (Equipment and Personnel)

           to protect against high concentrations of H2S gas expected through the Drilling Operation,

            details includes without limitation:

               1. To ensure all H2S equipment is in good condition.

               2. To supply, maintain, inspect, calibrate and repair all H2S equipment to the highest

                 standard and have this equipment ready for use all the time.

               3. To supply the gas detectors & required accessories to automate the gas detection

               program with capabilities of continually evaluate the condition of the gas monitor and

                 should be able to collect information and provide automate & manual alerts regarding

                  the status of the available toxics gases.

               4. To  review  Company’s  H2S  evacuation  procedure  and   to  advise  where

                 change/modifications are required.

               5. To monitor levels of H2S, inform Company, and take any necessary precautions to

                 safeguard life, the well and the equipment from impending danger.

               6. To customize safety training program.

               7. To maintain a minimum inventory of parts and accessories to facilitate ease and quality

                 provision of service.

               8. To prepare and submit detailed technical and quality reports on timely basis.

               9. To keep to be available at rig site on 24 hours/day.

              10. The supply of different types (fixed multi-sensor, portable, and electronic, and non-

                   electronic, sampling type) of H2S gas detection equipment to be installed, tested and

               commissioned on the rig that will perform the drilling, complete with dual level (10-

              20ppm) visual and audible alarms at different points of the rig site, to allow for early

                warning of all personnel on the rig for H2S gas occurrence in the atmosphere, hence

                 ease of following emergency plans to safety briefing areas. All equipment and devices

                   shall be on location before well SPUND-IN, service personnel shall be on location

                 before spud in the hole section which contain H2S or other poisonous gases.

              11. The supply of a comprehensive cascade breathing air loop system, complete with

          the Drills as per operational convenience.

        The Drills must consist of a dry-run performance of personnel activities related to assigned

           jobs.

        The Drills must include measures to ensure that:

               Personnel recognize the different alarms and respond correctly

               Personnel know the location of correct muster point or alternative upwind safe areas.

               Personnel muster in an orderly manner, as quickly as possible.

                Essential personnel report to their respective stations ready to carry out their duties.

           Drill organizers learn of deficiencies during Drills and make necessary improvement until

          proficiency is achieved.

      M.7. HSE and H2S

          This information sheet provides guidance  for duty-holders on detecting and managing

         hydrogen sulfide (H2S) hazards in hydrocarbon processing systems. As reservoirs begin to

          water out, hydrogen sulfide can become an issue when processing returning fluids. The gas is

           toxic in relatively low concentrations and the risks to the workforce need to be addressed as

         soon as its‟ presence becomes known. It is not intended to provide detailed advice on workplace

         exposure limits.

         Hydrogen sulfide is considered a broad-spectrum poison, meaning that it can poison several

           different systems in the body, although the nervous system and respiratory systems are most

           affected. Besides being highly toxic H2S is a flammable gas. It is heavier than air and hence

          tends to accumulate in low-lying areas. It is pungent but rapidly destroys the sense of smell.

        H2S is toxic at breathable concentrations between 500-1000ppm, but death is not instantaneous.

         However, at concentrations of greater than 1000ppm, H2S is rapidly lethal. Industry practice is

           to recognize that fatality from H2S exposure can occur over a wide concentration band but at

         around 500–1000ppm exposure for a short period, the  fatal exposure levels would be

           significant.

         800 ppm  is the generally accepted lethal concentration for 50% of an exposed human

          population for 5 minutes exposure (LC50).

        Some further details on concentrations and effects are given below:

               0.0047 ppm is the recognition threshold of human smell, the concentration at which

            50% of humans can detect the characteristic odor of hydrogen sulfide.

        March 2016                      Page 200 of 223          Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan

                  APP-04 Drilling Program

Doc No.: NDCAZD-EN-WP-004-0             H2S Monitoring & Control                   Contract No.: MTN-94-854

               10-20 ppm is the borderline concentration for eye irritation.

               50-100 ppm leads to eye damage.

               At 150-250 ppm the olfactory nerve is paralyzed after a few inhalations, and the sense

                 of smell disappears, often together with awareness of danger.

               320-530 ppm leads to pulmonary edema with the possibility of death.

               530-1000 ppm causes strong stimulation of the central nervous system and rapid

                  breathing, leading to loss of breathing.

               Concentrations over 1000 ppm cause immediate collapse with loss of breathing, even

                    after inhalation of a single breath.

       HSE sets limits for the control of hazardous substances in air. They are known as workplace

         exposure limits (WELs) and are set to help protect the health of workers who may be exposed

           to such substances. These limits are concentrations of hazardous substances in the air, averaged

          over a specific period referred to as a time-weighted average (TWA). Two time periods are

          used: long-term (8 hours) and short-term (15 minutes). Short-term exposure limits (STEL) are

            set to help prevent acute effects such as eye irritation, which may occur following exposure for

          a few minutes. HSE does not set limits or publish information on concentrations of gasses that

          are immediately dangerous to life or health (IDLH). However, the National Institute of Safety

         and Health (NIOSH) in the USA has set IDLH figures for a number of substances, which may

         be used by duty-holders.

      M.8. Standards and guidance

        ISO 10418 covers industry practice for handling H2S and is based on the accepted criterion that

           significant  fatalities would occur from exposure  to H2S  in gas  at  concentrations of

         500-1000ppm. Detection guidance is based on the argument that the hydrocarbon gas detection

         system will provide adequate detection if the H2S is below 500ppm in the hydrocarbon (HC)

          process streams. This is an acceptable argument only if it can be demonstrated that the HC

          detection system is ~100% effective. Above 500ppm specific H2S detection is required.

         Hydrogen sulfide has a WEL of 5 ppm 8-hour TWA based on data indicating a shift towards

          anaerobic respiration at exposures to 10 ppm.

        March 2016                      Page 201 of 223          Drilling Engineering Department

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan

                  APP-04 Drilling Program

Doc No.: NDCAZD-EN-WP-004-0             H2S Monitoring & Control                   Contract No.: MTN-94-854

       NIOSH has set a concentration of 100 ppm as IDLH.

        Use of hydrocarbon detection systems for H2S monitoring need to take account of this

          “detection effectiveness”.

      M.9. Hazard identification and management

          Production of liquid and gaseous hydrocarbons containing hydrogen sulfide in significant

         amounts can be hazardous to people. H2S in hydrocarbon fluids has the potential to form sulfur

          dioxide (SO2) via combustion. Flaring hydrocarbon gas containing measurable quantities of

        H2S needs to be managed to ensure the SO2 produced does not present a toxic hazard.

         Combinations of H2S, SO2 and hydrocarbon gaseous mixtures also need to be considered in

          terms of their accumulated toxic load.

        H2S presence in reservoir streams tends to increase with time and it is important therefore that

          duty-holders ensure that if the concentration levels in modules begin to increase the toxicity

           status of the areas are revised accordingly. The recommended strategy for fixed and portable

          detection is based on accepted management practice for confined space entry, and normally

           accessible process modules.

      M.10. Training Program

          All personnel working in an area where concentrations of Hydrogen Sulfide may exceed the

         10 Parts per Million (PPM) should be provided with training before beginning work

          assignments.

          All operators and contract personnel must complete site specific H2S Induction Training

         Program conducted by qualified H2S Engineer.
